# Cosmetic clay

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="cosmetic-clay-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="cosmetic-clay-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="cosmetic-clay-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Cosmetic clay in the HMI library has 104 reported results for lead, cadmium, total mercury, nickel, chromium, uranium, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, As, Hg, Th, Ti, Y and Zr from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total mercury, nickel, chromium and uranium and others, in 104 results from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the cosmetic clay was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>
</section>


<a id="decision-summary"></a>
## Cosmetic clay overview

<a id="hmi-ing-004"></a>
<a id="which-contamination-concerns-are-supported-for-this-ingredient-and-its-defined-forms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-005"></a>
<a id="which-sourcing-or-production-decisions-deserve-priority-on-the-available-evidence"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>
<a id="which-evidence-gaps-most-limit-those-decisions"></a>

Cosmetic clay in the HMI library has 104 reported results for lead, cadmium, total mercury, nickel, chromium, uranium, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, As, Hg, Th, Ti, Y and Zr from 2 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Fischer et al. 2025 reported total mercury at 23.7 µg/kg (median) in cosmetic clay, as sold from all products, Polish market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the cosmetic clay came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as cosmetic clay

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

This page covers cosmetic clay, as sold (104 results).<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<a id="heavy-metal-contamination-profile"></a>
## Heavy metal contamination profile of cosmetic clay

<a id="hmi-ing-007"></a>
<a id="which-metals-and-chemical-species-have-been-investigated-in-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-008"></a>
<a id="what-concentrations-detection-frequencies-and-variability-were-observed-in-each-defined-sampling-frame"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-009"></a>
<a id="which-analyte-questions-are-unassessed-access-blocked-conflicting-or-searched-without-eligible-evidence"></a>

The table holds every extracted cosmetic clay result: one row for each statistic a source reports for a form, group and metal, 104 rows in all, grouped by metal and form; percentiles, ranges and maxima for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Monitoring medians are HMI calculations from individual submissions; non-detects remain intervals between zero and the reporting limit and are never read as zero.

0 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 26 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-cosmetic-clay" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-cosmetic-clay-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of cosmetic clay, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

</div></details><p class="hmi-explorer-description" data-result-synopsis hidden><strong>First displayed analyte:</strong> <span data-result-synopsis-text>Lead: 2 findings from 1 source. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach cosmetic clay

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>
<a id="what-contamination-can-arise-from-agricultural-inputs-harvesting-processing-equipment-or-packaging"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared cosmetic clay from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.



<a id="ranges-by-source-region-and-variety"></a>
## Which forms and regions measured lower

<a id="hmi-ing-013"></a>
<a id="how-do-concentrations-vary-by-origin-growing-conditions-variety-and-harvest-period"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-014"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-distinguishes-within-lot-between-lot-supplier-and-production-system-variation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-015"></a>
<a id="which-comparisons-support-a-sourcing-decision-and-where-would-geographic-or-supplier-generalization-be-unjustified"></a>

In one study, total mercury across 17 groups of cosmetic clay ranged from 2.54 µg/kg (purple clay products, Polish market) to 194 µg/kg (green clay, manufacturer C); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for cosmetic clay

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit.

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.

No maximum level for metals in cosmetic clay was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from cosmetic clay

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>

No source in the library converted cosmetic clay concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Cosmetic clay FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-cosmetic-clay" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-cosmetic-clay" data-question="What heavy metals are found in cosmetic clay?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in cosmetic clay?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total mercury, nickel, chromium, uranium, Fe, Mn, Zn, V, Se, As, Hg, Th, Ti, Y and Zr in cosmetic clay, most often lead, cadmium and total mercury.<sup class="cite">[[sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">S&#39;busiso M Nkosi, Nokukhanya Thembane · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Physical, chemical and biological characteristics of clays from Durban (South Africa) for applications in cosmetics</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Analytical Science Advances</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/nkosi2024-durban-cosmetic-clays">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-origins-or-products-of-cosmetic-clay-measured-lowest" data-anchor="which-origins-or-products-of-cosmetic-clay-measured-lowest" data-question="Which origins or products of cosmetic clay measured lowest?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which origins or products of cosmetic clay measured lowest?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

In one study, total mercury across 17 groups of cosmetic clay ranged from 2.54 µg/kg (purple clay products, Polish market) to 194 µg/kg (green clay, manufacturer C); the lowest is the lowest observed in that study, not an established sourcing preference.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Fischer A, Brodziak-Dopierała B, Jańska W, Jeyranyan L, Malara B · 2025</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Analysis of Mercury Concentration in Cosmetic Clays</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Toxics 2025, 13, 507</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fischer2025-poland-cosmetic-clays-mercury">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
